## 3. Go — using `github.com/go-git/git` (wraps libgit2)

```go
// main.go
package main

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"

	"github.com/go-git/git"
)

type CloneResult struct {
	LocalDir string
}

func initGit(repoPath string) *git.Git {
	return git.New(git.WithPath(repoPath))
}

// cloneRepo clones `repoPath` into `localDir`.
func cloneRepo(repoPath, localDir string) error {
	if err := os.MkdirAll(localDir, 0755); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("mkdir %s: %w", localDir, err)
	}
	
	gitObj := initGit(repoPath)
	// git Clone <repo> [target]
	result, err := gitObj.Clone(git.WithTarget(localDir))
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("git clone failed: %w", err)
	}
	return result
}

// commitAndPush stages, commits and pushes.
func commitAndPush(repoPath, localDir string) error {
	gitObj := initGit(repoPath)
	
	// cd into clone & add
	cmd := exec.Command("git", "add", ".", "./"+localDir)
	cmd.Dir = localDir
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil || string(out) != "" {
		return fmt.Errorf("git add failed: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
	
	// commit
	cmd = exec.Command("git", "commit", "-m", "Initial commit")
	cmd.Dir = localDir
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil || string(out) != "" {
		return fmt.Errorf("git commit failed: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
	
	// push
	cmd = exec.Command("git", "push", "origin", "*")
	cmd.Dir = localDir
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil || string(out) != "" {
		return fmt.Errorf("git push failed: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
	
	return nil
}

func main() {
	// Example:
	// if err := commitAndPush("https://github.com/example/repo.git", "/tmp/aura-bench/session_11015/t53-03/my-clone"); err != nil {
	//     fmt.Printf("error: %v\n", err)
	// }
}
```

**Usage:**

```go
package main

import "fmt"

func main() {
	err := commitAndPush("https://github.com/example/repo.git", "/tmp/aura-bench/session_11015/t53-03/my-clone")
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Printf("error: %v\n", err)
		return
	}
	fmt.Println("push complete")
}
```

## Summary of APIs

| Language | Clone | Commit | Push |
|----------|-------|--------|------|
| **TypeScript** (`@libgit2/git2-wrapper-core`) | `git.execute('clone', ...)` via CLI wrapper or core API | `git.execute(...)` with `-m "msg"` | same as commit push |
| **Python** (via `subprocess` / gitpython) | `subprocess.run(["git", "clone", ...])` | `subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", "..."])` | `subprocess.run(["git", "push", "origin", "*"])` |
| **Go** (`github.com/go-git/git`) | `gitObj.Clone(git.WithTarget(localDir))` | `exec.Command("git commit ...")` + capture output | same as commit push |

All three follow the same logical flow:
1. **Clone** → create target directory, run `git clone`.
2. **Stage & Commit** → `git add .`, then `git commit -m "Initial commit"`.
3. **Push** → `git push origin *`.

> ⚠️ The TypeScript version assumes `@libgit2/git2-wrapper-core` and `@libgit2/git2-wrapper-cli` are installed via npm (`npm i @libgit2/git2-wrapper-core @libgit2/git2-wrapper-cli`). The Python version uses `subprocess` (no extra deps beyond stdlib). The Go version requires `github.com/go-git/git`.

## First compaction level: **55% of the context window**

The trigger ladder is `LADDER = [0.55, 0.70, 0.85]` (line 13), indexed by *recap generation* — how many times the current recap has already been compacted. `thresholdRatio(generation)` (line 28) returns `LADDER[min(generation, 2)]`, so:

| Generation | Trigger at | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 (never compacted) | **55%** | first compaction |
| 1 | 70% | second round |
| 2 | 85% | third round |
| 3+ | 85% (capped) | then `ROLLOVER_AT_GENERATION = 3` says stop recompacting in place and do a full generational flush instead |

The check itself is in `compactHistory` (lines 240–245): `threshold = floor(window * thresholdRatio(generation))`, and it bails out with `false` if `totalTokens < threshold`. The window comes from `getContextWindow(model)`, defaulting to `DEFAULT_WINDOW = 128_000` (line 19) — so on an unknown model the first compaction fires at ~70,400 tokens.

Two details worth knowing about the measurement: `totalTokens` is the *estimated next-prompt size* (`estimateContextTokens`, line 102 — system prompt + full history), not a sum of provider `usage` figures; and it's counted with `gpt-tokenizer` when installed, otherwise a deliberately conservative 3.5 chars/token (line 57), so it errs toward compacting slightly early.

## What it keeps vs. summarizes

**Kept verbatim:**
1. **The first message** — `history[0]`, the original task (line 276).
2. **A recent tail** of up to `RETENTION_RATIO = 0.40` — 40% of the window — computed by `computeTailBoundary` (line 200). It walks backward accumulating message sizes until the budget is hit, then snaps the boundary to the nearest user-turn start (within 6 messages) so the kept slice opens with user context and no `tool_use`/`tool_result` pair is split. If no user turn is nearby, it falls back to keeping at least `FALLBACK_KEEP = 3` messages and never starts the slice on an orphaned `tool_result`.
3. **The prior recap's accumulated body** — everything below the `--- compacted turns ---` marker is carried forward byte-for-byte (lines 255–261); only the newly aged-out turns get appended. That keeps the recap a stable prefix so cache breakpoints after it survive.

**Summarized (the middle, `history.slice(1, keepFrom)`):** each message becomes one line via `summariseMessage` (line 149) — user text truncated to 120 chars, assistant text truncated to 120 chars plus the names of tools it called, and tool results reduced to just `Tool results: [names]` with the bodies dropped entirely. These lines are appended under the recap header, which is regenerated each call and carries the generation number, an optional affect/tone hint, and an optional executive digest.

**Also:** a churn guard (`truncateOversizedResults`, line 292) caps any single tool result at `MAX_RESULT_CHARS = 4_000` if the history is *still* over threshold after compaction — otherwise one huge result in the kept tail would re-trigger compaction every turn with no shrinkage. And `compactHistory` refuses to act at all when `history.length <= 3` (line 245).
